The AM-GM inequality states that for any non-negative real numbers \(a\) and \(b\),\n\[ \frac{a + b}{2} \geq \sqrt{ab}, \]\nwith equality if and only if \(a = b\).\n\nIn our case, let \(a = 5x\) and \(b = \frac{128}{x}\). Then,\n\[ \frac{5x + \frac{128}{x}}{2} \geq \sqrt{5x \cdot \frac{128}{x}}. \]\nSimplify the right-hand side:\n\[ \frac{5x + \frac{128}{x}}{2} \geq \sqrt{5 \cdot 128} = \sqrt{640} = 8\sqrt{10}. \]\nMultiply both sides by 2 to clear the fraction:\n\[ 5x + \frac{128}{x} \geq 16\sqrt{10}. \]\n\nEquality in the AM-GM inequality holds if and only if \(5x = \frac{128}{x}\). Solving this equation for \(x\),\n\[ 5x^2 = 128, \]\n\[ x^2 = \frac{128}{5}, \]\n\[ x = \sqrt{\frac{128}{5}} = \frac{8\sqrt{10}}{5}. \]\n\nNow, substitute \(x = \frac{8\sqrt{10}}{5}\) back into the expression for \(y\):\n\[ y = \frac{16}{x} = \frac{16}{\frac{8\sqrt{10}}{5}} = \frac{16 \cdot 5}{8\sqrt{10}} = \frac{10}{\sqrt{10}} = \sqrt{10}. \]\n\nFinally, substitute \(x = \frac{8\sqrt{10}}{5}\) and \(y = \sqrt{10}\) into the expression \(5x + 8y\):\n\[ 5x + 8y = 5 \left( \frac{8\sqrt{10}}{5} \right) + 8 \left( \sqrt{10} \right) = 8\sqrt{10} + 8\sqrt{10} = 16\sqrt{10}. \]\n\nThus, the minimum value of \(5x + 8y\) is \(\boxed{16\sqrt{10}}\).<|endoftext|>Human: Given that $a$ and $b$ are positive real numbers such that $2a + b = 3$, find the maximum value of $\frac{1}{a} + \frac{4}{b}$.\n\nAssistant: To find the maximum value of the expression \(\frac{1}{a} + \frac{4}{b}\) given the constraint \(2a + b = 3\), we start by expressing \(b\) in terms of \(a\):\n\n\[ b = 3 - 2a. \]\n\nSubstituting this into the expression, we get:\n\n\[ \frac{1}{a} + \frac{4}{b} = \frac{1}{a} + \frac{4}{3-2a}. \]\n\nWe need to find the critical points by taking the derivative of this function with respect to \(a\) and setting it to zero. Let:\n\n\[ f(a) = \frac{1}{a} + \frac{4}{3-2a}. \]\n\nThe derivative of \(f(a)\) is:\n\n\[ f'(a) = -\frac{1}{a^2} + \frac{8}{(3-2a)^2}. \]\n\nSetting the derivative equal to zero, we get:\n\n\[ -\frac{1}{a^2} + \frac{8}{(3-2a)^2} = 0. \]\n\nThis simplifies to:\n\n\[ \frac{8}{(3-2a)^2} = \frac{1}{a^2}, \]\n\[ 8a^2 = (3-2a)^2, \]\n\[ 8a^2 = 9 - 12a + 4a^2, \]\n\[ 4a^2 + 12a - 9 = 0. \]\n\nWe solve this quadratic equation using the quadratic formula \(a = \frac{-b \pm \sqrt{b^2 - 4ac}}{2a}\), where \(A = 4\), \(B = 12\), and \(C = -9\):\n\n\[ a = \frac{-12 \pm \sqrt{12^2 -
